## datasync-faq-interrupted-task-next-run-completes [mech-pass r1 behavior hard=True rel=core vol=stable chg=-]
C: If a DataSync task is interrupted, for example by a network failure or an agent restart, the next run transfers the missing files and the data is complete and consistent at the end of that run, because each run is an incremental copy of the changes.
D: the next run of the task will transfer missing files
Q: scope=AWS DataSync tasks; dom=; cond=
E: www/datasync/faqs :: If a task is interrupted, for instance, if the network connection goes down or the AWS DataSync agent is restarted, the next run of the task will transfer missing files, and the data will be complete and consistent at the end of this run. Each time a task is started it performs an incremental copy, transferring only the changes from the source to the destination.

## storage-gateway-faq-s3-file-gateway-cache-holds-changed-and-recent-data [mech-pass r1 behavior hard=True rel=core vol=stable chg=-]
C: The local disk of an S3 File Gateway temporarily holds changed data waiting to be transferred to AWS and caches data for low-latency reads. The gateway manages the cache itself and evicts data only when space is needed for more recently used data.
D: temporarily hold changed data || evicted from the cache only when space
Q: scope=S3 File Gateway cache; dom=; cond=
E: www/storagegateway/faqs :: Local disk storage on the gateway is used to temporarily hold changed data that needs to be transferred to AWS, and to locally cache data for low-latency read access. File Gateway automatically manages the cache maintaining the most recently accessed data based on client read and write operations. Data is evicted from the cache only when space is needed to store more recently used data.

## storage-gateway-faq-s3-file-gateway-cache-sizing-guidance [mech-pass r1 behavior hard=False rel=extended vol=stable chg=-]
C: Provision the S3 File Gateway cache based on the size of the working dataset that needs low-latency access and on the size of the files that applications write to the gateway.
D: size of your working dataset || size of files written to the gateway
Q: scope=S3 File Gateway cache; dom=; cond=
E: www/storagegateway/faqs :: You should provision your cache based on:
1/ The size of your working dataset to which you need low-latency access, so you can reduce read latencies by decreasing the frequency with which data is requested from S3, and
2/ The size of files written to the gateway by your applications.

E: www/storagegateway/faqs :: All data transferred between the gateway and AWS storage is encrypted using SSL. By default, all data stored in S3 is encrypted server-side with Amazon S3-Managed Encryption Keys (SSE-S3). For each file share you can optionally configure to have your objects encrypted with AWS KMS-Managed Keys using SSE-KMS.

E: www/storagegateway/faqs :: If the cache is too small then read latencies will increase as data being requested must be fetched from S3, and writes could fail if there is no free cache space to store data locally pending upload to S3.

D: 2026-09-15 || replaced the client's source IP with its own private IP address
Q: scope=SFTP servers with a VPC-hosted endpoint behind a Network Load Balancer; dom=; cond=enabled per server
E: docs/transfer/latest/userguide/doc-history :: | Source IP preservation for SFTP servers behind a Network Load Balancer | AWS Transfer Family SFTP servers behind a Network Load Balancer (NLB) can use PROXY protocol v2 to preserve the client's source IP address. IP-based access policies in your custom identity provider then see the real client address instead of the NLB private IP. For details, see [Working with Network Load Balancers]. | Septe [+1 ev]
